=== idx: 932 ===
Gold supporting: transcription - coupled repair ( tcr ) is generally observed as more rapid or more efficient removal of certain types of dna damage from the transcribed strands of expressed genes compared with the nontranscribed strands.
LLM supporting:  transcription - coupled repair ( tcr ) primarily targets the non - template ( coding ) dna strand. this is because tcr is a subpathway of nucleotide excision repair ( ner ) that is specifically activated when dna damage occurs in the transcribed region of genes. the repair process is coupled with transcription, meaning it is initiated and resolved while the gene is being transcribed. this mechanism helps to prevent the replication of damaged dna and ensures the integrity of the genetic information being expressed. tcr involves the coordinated action of the ner pathway and the rna polymerase complex, which are essential for recognizing and repairing the dna lesions that block or distort the transcription machinery. the specific targeting of the non - template strand is crucial for maintaining the fidelity of the transcribed mrna and, ultimately, the functional proteins produced from it.

=== idx: 983 ===
Gold supporting: murine g9a is a 1263 amino acid h3 - k9 methyltransferase that possesses characteristic set domain and ank repeats.
LLM supporting:  histone methyltransferases, such as g9a, are characterized by the presence of specific domains that mediate their catalytic and regulatory functions. the n - terminus of g9a contains nuclear localization signals ( nls ) which are essential for the enzyme to enter the nucleus and access its target, chromatin. this region is critical for the proper targeting of g9a to the nuclear compartment where it can methylate histone h3, particularly at lysine 9 ( h3 - k9 ). additionally, the c - terminus of g9a, including the catalytic core, is responsible for the actual methylation reaction. this domain includes conserved residues that are involved in the binding of adenosine methionine ( adomet ), the methyl donor, and in the catalytic process. the distinction between these n - and c - terminal domains highlights how g9a, and potentially other histone methyltransferases, are structured to perform their dual roles of nuclear targeting and catalysis. importantly, the c - terminus, especially the catalytic region, is not essential for nuclear targeting but is crucial for the enzymatic activity. thus, the characteristic domain of histone methyltransferases, as exemplified by g9a, includes both n - and c - terminal regions, each with specialized functions.
